```python
n = int(input())
probabilities = list(map(float, input().split()))

# Best probability from a single friend
best_single = max(probabilities)

# Probability of exactly one success when all friends are asked
all_prob = 0.0
product_of_failures = 1.0

# We can compute all_prob by iterating and for each friend, multiply by (1 - p_j) for j != i
# But it's easier to compute as: for each i, p_i * (product of (1 - p_j) for j != i)
for i in range(n):
    failure_product = 1.0
    for j in range(n):
        if i != j:
            failure_product *= (1.0 - probabilities[j])
    all_prob += probabilities[i] * failure_product

# The answer is the maximum of taking one friend and taking all friends
answer = max(best_single, all_prob)

print(f"{answer:.12f}")
```

This should work for the given examples and likely for the test cases. The time complexity is O(n^2) which is acceptable for n ≤ 100.

n = int(input())
probabilities = list(map(float, input().split()))

# Best probability from a single friend
best_single = max(probabilities)

# Probability of exactly one success when all friends are asked
all_prob = 0.0
product_of_failures = 1.0

# We can compute all_prob by iterating and for each friend, multiply by (1 - p_j) for j != i
# But it's easier to compute as: for each i, p_i * (product of (1 - p_j) for j != i)
for i in range(n):
    failure_product = 1.0
    for j in range(n):
        if i != j:
            failure_product *= (1.0 - probabilities[j])
    all_prob += probabilities[i] * failure_product

# The answer is the maximum of taking one friend and taking all friends
answer = max(best_single, all_prob)

print(f"{answer:.12f}")

You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given a string S consisting of lowercase English letters. Determine whether all the characters in S are different.

-----Constraints-----
 - 2 ≤ |S| ≤ 26, where |S| denotes the length of S.
 - S consists of lowercase English letters.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
S

-----Output-----
If all the characters in S are different, print yes (case-sensitive); otherwise, print no.

-----Sample Input-----
uncopyrightable

-----Sample Output-----
yes### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string S consisting of lowercase English letters
2. We need to determine if all characters in the string are different (no duplicates)
3. If all characters are different, output "yes", otherwise output "no"
4. The string length is between 2 and 26 characters

## Approach

There are several ways to solve this:

1. **Using a set**: Convert the string to a set and compare the length of the set with the length of the original string. If they're equal, all characters are unique.

2. **Using a loop with a flag**: Iterate through each character and check if we've seen it before.

3. **Using built-in functions**: Use `len(set(string)) == len(string)`

The set approach is the most efficient and concise.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the input string S
2. Convert S to a set to get unique characters
3. Compare the length of the set with the length of the original string
4. If they are equal, all characters are different → print "yes"
5. Otherwise, there are duplicates → print "no"

## Verification with Sample

Input: "uncopyrightable"
- Length of string: 16
- Unique characters: u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b, e, l (let me count: "uncopyrightable" has 16 unique characters)
- Since length of set equals length of string, all characters are different → "yes"

This matches the expected output.

## Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()
if len(set(S)) == len(S):
    print("yes")
else:
    print("no")
```

The solution works because:
- `set(S)` creates a set of unique characters from string S
- If all characters are different, the set will have the same number of elements as the original string
- If there are duplicates, the set will have fewer elements than the original string

    print("yes")
else:
    print("no")

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string S consisting of lowercase English letters
2. We need to determine if all characters in the string are different (no duplicates)
3. If all characters are different, output "yes", otherwise output "no"
4. Constraints: string length is between 2 and 26

## Approach

There are several ways to solve this:

**Method 1: Use a set to track seen characters**
- Iterate through each character in the string
- Keep a set of characters we've already seen
- If we encounter a character that's already in the set, there's a duplicate
- If we finish the loop without finding duplicates, all characters are unique

**Method 2: Compare length of string with length of set**
- Since a set only stores unique elements, if the length of the original string equals the length of the set of characters, then all characters are unique
- This is more concise and efficient

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the input string S
2. Convert the string to a set to get unique characters
3. Compare the length of the original string with the length of the set
4. If they're equal, all characters are different → print "yes"
5. If they're not equal, there are duplicates → print "no"

## Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()

if len(S) == len(set(S)):
    print("yes")
else:
    print("no")
```

Actually, let me double-check: "uncopyrightable" has 16 characters total.

The algorithm will work regardless - if all characters are different, the set length will equal the original length.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string S of lowercase English letters
2. We need to determine if all characters in the string are different (no duplicates)
3. If all characters are different, print "yes", otherwise print "no"
4. The string length is between 2 and 26 characters

## Approach Analysis

There are several ways to solve this:

**Method 1: Using a set**
- Convert the string to a set and compare the length of the set with the length of the original string
- If they're equal, all characters are unique
- If they're different, there are duplicates

**Method 2: Using a loop and a seen set**
- Iterate through each character and keep track of characters we've seen
- If we encounter a character we've already seen, return "no"
- If we finish the loop without duplicates, return "yes"

**Method 3: Using built-in functions**
- Use `len(set(S)) == len(S)` to check if all characters are unique

The set method is the most concise and efficient for this problem.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the input string S
2. Convert S to a set to get unique characters
3. Compare the length of the set with the length of the original string
4. If they're equal, all characters are different → print "yes"
5. If they're different, there are duplicates → print "no"

## Verification with Sample

Sample input: "uncopyrightable"
- Length of string: 15
- Characters: u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b, l, e
- All these characters are different
- Set of characters has 15 elements
- 15 == 15, so answer is "yes" ✓

## Code Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()
if len(set(S)) == len(S):
    print("yes")
else:
    print("no")
```

The solution works because:
- `set(S)` creates a set of unique characters from string S
- `len(set(S))` gives us the count of unique characters
- If all characters in S are different, the unique count will equal the original length
- If there are duplicates, the unique count will be less than the original length

The time complexity is O(n) where n is the length of the string, and space complexity is O(1) since we have at most 26 unique lowercase letters.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string S of lowercase English letters
2. We need to determine if all characters in the string are different (no duplicates)
3. If all characters are different, print "yes", otherwise print "no"

## Analysis

Since the string only contains lowercase English letters (a-z) and the length is at most 26, we can use a simple approach:

- If all characters are different, then the length of the string should equal the length of the set of characters
- A set automatically removes duplicates, so if the original string length equals the length of the set, all characters are unique

## Step-by-step approach

1. Read the input string S
2. Convert the string to a set to get unique characters
3. Compare the length of the original string with the length of the set
4. If they are equal, all characters are different → print "yes"
5. If they are different, there are duplicates → print "no"

## Example verification

For "uncopyrightable":
- Original length: 16
- Characters: u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b, l, e
- All these characters are unique (no repeats)
- Set length: 16
- Since 16 == 16, output is "yes" ✓

## Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()
if len(S) == len(set(S)):
    print("yes")
else:
    print("no")
```

The solution works because:
- `len(S)` gives us the total number of characters
- `set(S)` creates a set of unique characters from the string
- `len(set(S))` gives us the count of unique characters
- If all characters are different, these two values will be equal
- If there are duplicates, the set length will be less than the original length

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

You are given a string S consisting of lowercase English letters. Determine whether all the characters in S are different.

-----Constraints-----
 - 2 ≤ |S| ≤ 26, where |S| denotes the length of S.
 - S consists of lowercase English letters.

-----Input-----
Input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
S

-----Output-----
If all the characters in S are different, print yes (case-sensitive); otherwise, print no.

-----Sample Input-----
uncopyrightable

-----Sample Output-----
yes### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

### Answer: (use the provided format with backticks)

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step-by-step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a string S of lowercase English letters
2. We need to determine if all characters in the string are different (no duplicates)
3. If all characters are different, print "yes", otherwise print "no"
4. The length of S is between 2 and 26 characters

## Approach Analysis

Since we only have lowercase English letters (26 total), and the string length is at most 26, we can use several approaches:

**Approach 1: Use a set to track seen characters**
- Iterate through each character in the string
- Keep a set of characters we've already seen
- If we encounter a character already in the set, we have a duplicate
- If we complete the iteration without finding duplicates, all characters are unique

**Approach 2: Compare string length with length of unique characters**
- If the string length equals the length of the set of characters, then all are unique
- Otherwise, there are duplicates

Both approaches work, but Approach 1 is more straightforward and clear.

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Read the input string S
2. Create an empty set to track seen characters
3. Iterate through each character in S
4. For each character:
   - If it's already in the seen set, return "no" (duplicate found)
   - Otherwise, add it to the seen set
5. If we complete the iteration without finding duplicates, return "yes"

## Edge Cases to Consider

- Strings of length 2: both characters must be different
- Strings with repeated characters at different positions
- The constraint guarantees that we won't have more than 26 characters, so duplicates are possible

## Implementation

```python
S = input().strip()

seen = set()
for char in S:
    if char in seen:
        print("no")
        exit()
    seen.add(char)

print("yes")
```

## Verification with Sample

Input: "uncopyrightable"

- u: not seen → add to set {u}
- n: not seen → add to set {u, n}
- c: not seen → add to set {u, n, c}

- p: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p}
- y: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y}
- r: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r}
- i: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i}
- g: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g}
- h: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h}
- t: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t}
- a: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a}
- b: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b}
- l: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b, l}
- e: not seen → add to set {u, n, c, o, p, y, r, i, g, h, t, a, b, l, e}

All characters are unique, so output is "yes" ✓

The solution correctly handles the problem requirements.
